This spec owns the items that change existing code.\n\n## Goal & Context\n\nThe model workspace (~86k lines of Rust) hand-rolls a few things that admitted or widely used crates do, and repeats several small helpers across crates. This spec replaces those with crates or one shared copy, moves hand-rolled golden comparisons onto insta, and drops the binary-snapshot layout that forced the fixture runner to decode insta's private file naming. Checker semantics, Model meaning and the semantic protocol contract are preserved; internal and wire representations may change with coordinated regeneration. Prost-build's default WKT mappings replace in-house well-known-type generation consistently across runtime, Kettle and shared Temporal codegen: nonprimitive types use `prost-types`, Empty uses unit and scalar/string/bytes wrappers use the corresponding Rust primitives; descriptor-derived paths use the same mapping. A thin wrapper keeps the Malformed-versus-Unsupported refusal split of `any.rs`. Recursion-only matches use them: `Expr::infallible` (only its non-recursive `false` arms stay explicit), `umpire-tla` `names::rename` and `emit::prime`, the fixtures' interpreted `rewrite`.             oneof,\n12:Current layout after R41: the Rust workspace is `model/`, the frozen Scala reference is `model/archived/`, and the local gates are `make model-check` and `make model-time`. Earlier conversation, migration instructions and decision records retain their original path names.\n14:Before any more of the Scala framework is built out, the owner wants to know whether Rust is the better base: it would make lifting a macro's by-product, make wasm and native hosting first-class, run the checker fast by default, and has more mind share at the company. The way to know is a bounded prototype, not an argument. `temporal/model` is a Cargo workspace mirroring `model/archived/framework`'s layout (`framework/dsl`, `framework/checker`, `framework/samples`). Its scope is exactly what `model/archived/framework/samples/Kettle.scala` uses today: vars with a `oneOf` start, actors with actions and internal steps, transitions with guards and effects, invariants, postconditions with `before` and `after(action)`, one `without` scope, one `whenever … eventually … in` progress claim, and the questions `explore`, `verifyAll` and `refuted`. The authoring-surface options were written out in `framework/samples/options/`; the owner chose to build the plain-trait engine first (option D, which every surface expands to) and then the attribute surface (option C, plain functions with pass-through attributes). Every other open spec is deferred until this prototype's write-up gives a verdict.\n19:- `framework-checker`: BFS exploration from the starts with a shared visited set and a state budget; per-state invariant evaluation; per-edge postcondition evaluation with `before`, `after` and the action; progress as a search for a fair lasso inside the scope's action set with weak fairness on internal steps; `Result` with verdict (verified, refuted with a path, unknown on budget cut), reached-state and emitted-step counts; `Report` from `verify_all` with per-claim results and vacuity warnings (an action never fired, an `after` claim never triggered); `refuted(claim)` for negative controls.\n26:- `Checker::explore::<M>(budget) -> Result<M>`; `Result::verify_all() -> Report<M>`; `Checker::refuted::<M>(claim) -> Result<M>`.\n35:- Progress within a scope excludes the scoped-out actions from the search entirely; fairness applies to internal steps only, as in the Scala checker's default.\n43:- **R2:** `Checker::explore` reaches exactly the Scala note's Kettle counts (81 starts, 283 states, depth 62) and `verify_all` reports the two invariant and postcondition claims verified with no vacuity warnings. Errors: a budget below one refuses; a cut yields unknown with the counts so far.\n45:- **R4:** `Checker::refuted` reports `!beeping` refuted with a path from a start to a beeping state. No error surface beyond R2.\n49:- **R8:** (2026-10-18) Tests are cases with golden files, not unit assertions. Behaviour: one test runs every registered sample Model and snapshots `cases/<model>/expected/report.txt` (starts, reached states, emitted steps, depth, cut, every claim with its verdict, vacuity warnings) and `cases/<model>/expected/paths/<claim>.txt` for each counterexample, with `insta`. Refusals: `trybuild` `.rs` + `.stderr`. Expansion: `macrotest` `.expanded.rs`. Goldens are deterministic (no hash-order leakage; states sorted, paths in BFS order over a fixed action order, a stable `Display` for states), human-readable (one claim or one step per line), contain no timings, and are updated by one flag wrapped as `make model-update`; a golden without a case or a case without a golden fails. One property test (proptest) checks the checker against a brute-force reference explorer on small random Models; it is the only check that goldens are right rather than merely stable. No other unit tests unless a defect cannot be expressed as a case. Errors: a nondeterministic golden (differs across two runs) is a defect of the producer, not of the case.\n52:- **R11:** (2026-10-18) `overflow-checks = true` in every profile; the checker runs each step under `catch_unwind`, and an overflow or panic in an effect refutes that step with its path, action and message, as the Scala checker treats a thrown effect. Errors: a panic in a claim is a defect of the claim and is reported the same way.\n56:- **R15:** (2026-10-18) `framework-dsl` and `framework-checker` build for `wasm32-wasip2` as a step of `make model-check`; a dependency on threads, the filesystem or the clock in either crate fails the gate. Errors: after the move no `model2` path remains outside history, and `make model-check` passes.\n88:- **R44:** (2026-10-18, owner approved; amended 2026-10-19 to the flat form after the nested and marker-trait forms were judged too much ceremony) A Model is one inline `mod` carrying `#[machine]` (`kettle::Model`), holding the `#[derive(State)]` struct, the `#[derive(Action)]` enums and one inherent `impl Model` whose methods carry per-item kind attributes: `#[on(Actor)]` (one `match` method per actor, match guards as the guards), `#[effect]` (optional helpers that may take `&mut self`), `#[invariant]`, `#[postcondition]` / `#[postcondition(after = Actor::Action)]`, `#[assumption]`, `#[progress]`. `#[machine]` enforces grouping and order: within the impl, items of one kind are contiguous and the kinds appear in that order; the check is a hard error at the first out-of-place item naming the kind it must follow and precede; unattributed helper methods are ignored by the order check but must not take `&mut self` (only `#[on]` and `#[effect]` may). Catalog order is the `Action` enums' declaration order; `#[machine]` takes no arguments and emits `#[forbid(unsafe_code)]` on the module. Every attribute is pass-through (R36); nothing is rewritten. Errors: trybuild `.stderr` fixtures pin the out-of-order, `&mut self`-outside-transitions and leftover-group-attribute messages.
